Differences from the Standard Ebooks edition

408 places where se-llm differs from SE, with 446 separate changes: 396 in the words and 50 in the markup only. Each place shows the same passage three ways as source, with markup and line breaks: the Gutenberg HTML on top, the SE XHTML in the middle and the se-llm XHTML at the bottom. SE and se-llm are highlighted character by character where they differ, tags included; a red bar marks where one side has nothing. In Gutenberg the highlight is the passage behind a text change. Next to each place, every change has its own cell: what differs, the se-llm step that made se-llm's version, and a verdict. Tags are compared by element and by epub:type, xml:lang, class and role; ids and link targets are ignored. Verdicts are a model's judgment (Claude Sonnet) against the SE Manual of Style and se-llm's keep-the-Gutenberg-reading policy; markup changes with the same tags share one verdict. They are not proof.

					<span​>I stiffen the bunt of her sail.”</span>
				</p>
			</blockquote>
		</section>

The i2 class on the first line only sets indentation, and the Gutenberg snippet does not show that indentation, so SE's i2 may be an editorial choice. The blockquote type (z3998:poem vs z3998:verse) is a similar judgment call for a quoted poem. se-llm does keep the nested-quote spacing (“ ‘).
text Gutenberg ‘ · SE – · se-llm ‘
se-llm kept the Gutenberg reading; SE changed it
se-llm step: kept from the Gutenberg draft
se-llm correct quotation marks
Gutenberg has a nested inner quote mark in front of 'Here at least', and se-llm keeps it. SE drops it, which loses the nested quotation in the poem.
